## v449 (2026-11-20) — Mothlight Museum 1.5: fair active-time search ranking (BUILD ONLY)

**Exactly one priority-1 scoring-integrity objective.** Original v448 Mothlight used wall-clock `performance.now() - start` from initial game load, so reading instructions or backgrounding the tab penalized a later fully earned 15-exhibit case. In deterministic real Chromium with 31 seconds foreground play and 120 seconds inactive, v448 recorded 151 seconds and 6,596 points rather than a 31-second result. Case progression remained valid; the score duration was unfair. No multiplayer/global-score compromise asserted.

**Original correction:** ranked timer now begins with the first real search action (cursor movement, object inspection, or paid hint) and accumulates only active foreground segments. `blur` and `visibilitychange` freeze a segment exactly once, overlapping `focus` cannot resume while hidden, and idle reading is free. Input cannot collect items, spend hints or move the cursor while inactive. The 1.8-second paid hint glow uses active time so the hint is not consumed offscreen. Original three randomized rooms, 15 earned exhibit finds, three room bonuses, decoys/misses, UI disclosures, score formula, keyboard/touch and replay remain unchanged. `case-closed` telemetry retains a single legitimate score event.

**Measured A/B:** controlled `performance.now()` and synthetic tab events within actual Chromium DOM/canvas: v448 151 sec / 6,596 pts after foreground search + 120 sec hidden; v449 31 sec / 7,076 pts for the identical play, with no double-count. Reading instructions 150 seconds before beginning now gives 1 active second / 7,196 pts; the v448 wall-clock result was 6,600 pts. Focused v449 54/54 including desktop 1280×800, emulated mobile 390×844, compact touch 320×240, overlapping blur/visibility, paid hint lifetime, inactivity input guard and real 15-item case completion. Historical Mothlight keyboard 4/4 and compact 91/91. ## v448 (2026-11-19) — Echo Bazaar 1.5: one-unit standing limit orders (BUILD ONLY)

**Exactly one objective (playability/strategic depth):** add a consequential planned-trade action to the existing 12-day/4-commodity trading game, rather than more content or a timing-only patch. Before: v447 had only immediate market buy/sell and no future conditional execution. After: players may queue one buy at/below an integer credit limit or one sell at/above that limit, for settlement at an actual **future day’s closing price**, with explicit state, cancelation and end-of-campaign expiry. Immediate trading, daily seeded prices/rumors, cost-basis, trade history, cargo capacity, and ranked profitable 12-day completion are preserved.

**Accounting/security boundary:** pending buy reserves limit credits and one cargo slot; escrow is always included in net worth, even while excluded from available cash. On fill the actual quote is charged and any price improvement refunded. Pending sell locks exactly one owned unit against manual sale until fill/cancel/expiry. Limit input requires a finite integer 18–400; no duplicate fill, overbooking, ghost cargo, bonus scoring, or paid API is introduced. At the 12th closing bell any unfilled order expires; there is no remote or shared order-book authority, and no real-money trading.

**Parent/parallel boundary:** isolated from immutable verified v447 `WorldWideGames_v447.zip` SHA-256 `021e7a03fddfeb4a101665bd0241ad52c0a18e5fcf17c7450d7cd945b1ef48da` (archive parent, **not** a Git merge base). ## v447 (2026-11-19) — Solar Loom 1.4: proven three-transition habitat (BUILD ONLY)

## v443 (2026-11-19) — Stoneveil Ascent 1.3: frame-fair summit timing (BUILD ONLY)

**Exactly one priority-1 competitive fairness objective:** in verified immutable v442, the Stoneveil timer charged full visible elapsed time while grip animation advanced only a maximum 33 ms per rendering callback. First-party Chromium with scripted animation callbacks reproduced a hold still unfinished after 500 ms at 10 Hz or 15 Hz, while 30–120 Hz players reached the same grip; each paid about 500 ms of scored time. The cause is a mismatch between animation progress and scored foreground elapsed time, not a claim about real measured hardware FPS.

**Original repair:** a bounded 2,000 ms foreground timeline advances climbing travel, belay/route-transition locks, and the eligible ranked clock in chronological order. A callback cannot pre-spend the transition duration before reaching the grip. Inactive-page pauses freeze both physics and time, held keyboard controls remain one-shot, fall/chalk costs unchanged, and only an earned fifth wall yields a single ranked summit event. Exceptional stalls above two seconds remain outside certified pacing parity. No external game source, assets, packages or money imported. ## v432 BUILD ONLY — Chronofold Courier 1.3: Fair Active-Play Archive Clock (2026-11-17)

**One bounded priority-1 scoring-integrity objective.** On the verified v431 original, `start=performance.now()` began at page load. The final `archive-delivered` competitive score used total wall time, deducting 7 points per second even during pre-play instruction reading and hidden-tab/blurred-window absence. First-party Chromium with a controlled monotonic clock reproduced exactly **840 fewer points** after an artificial 120-second pre-play or midgame hidden interval, at 1280×800, 390×844 and 320×240. This is simulated clock advancement inside real Chromium, not a physical two-minute wait.

**Original repair:** Chronofold 1.3 starts the scored clock on the first playable turn, accrues foreground time only, and suspends on page visibility or window blur without double-counting overlapping inactive events. Inactive turn/echo/reset inputs are ignored; focus/visibility resumes play. Restart resets the timer. The original five solvable stages, puzzle geometry, echoes, scoring weights, score-event identity, keyboard arrows/WASD, native touch dock and restart rules were preserved. Before/after A/B Chromium: 18/18 baseline checks reproduced the defect; 22/22 focused candidate checks passed. Five-stage input-driven historical Chromium 46/46 and mobile/compact layout historical 76/76 pass against the working candidate; these are controlled first-party tests rather than human gameplay. Final extracted-artifact evidence and SHA-256 hashes are in independently packaged sidecars, not preclaimed here.

## v425 BUILD ONLY — Hushwave Operator 1.7 — Phase-Accurate Signal Lock (2026-11-17)

**One reproducible gameplay/score integrity defect:** sealed v424 first-party Chromium showed phase 0 and 100 generate the same mathematical sine-wave trace (maximum sampled y difference <1.2e-13 px) but received unequal lock scores on all six carriers: 6%/56%, 57%/4%, 0%/69%, 0%/19%, 54%/0%, 0%/28%, respectively. The player-visible interference/noise also incorrectly changed because it was based on those unequal lock values. A periodic 2π signal requires the shortest circular phase difference, not the absolute difference of linear slider positions.

**Original repair:** `phaseDistance(a,b)=min(|a-b|,100-|a-b|)` now controls both the displayed lock/score rule and the "furthest from coherence" hint. All six 0/100 endpoint pairs now have equal lock scores and identical rendered canvas pixels in Chromium. Real frequency/gain tuning, difficulty tolerances, 86% threshold, attempt accounting, progression, achievement event, one-time score submission, touch and keyboard controls remain unchanged. User-facing instructions clarify that phase wraps. Focused tests use controlled stage indices for endpoint equivalence plus a native six-carrier button/slider completion; not unassisted human play.

**Candidate and isolation:** exact v424 Library source parent SHA-256 `3dfee5f138b0de8ebf23eeea6f5fb28eef0ee131a84cb431bbf04abd141ca21f`. Version v425; 83 games / 129 unique genres / 156 achievements / 55 remappable titles unchanged, all 82 other registered game executables (including Cyber Wars) byte-identical. Archive parentage is *not* a Git merge base. Canonical GitHub main observed read-only at `37f48c6ab75c47e89050ec7ee467cc87c0e8258a` (historical v72), specialist PR #1 draft/unmerged. Separate Flagship F3D58 and Cyber Wars integration candidates not imported. No repository/PR/main/tag writes and no Vercel production action.

## v385 — Rootsong Architect 1.1: Stage-Transition Integrity (BUILD ONLY; 2026-11-11)

**Exactly one score/progression-critical defect fixed.** Exact v384 allowed R during Rootsong's 650ms stage-complete intermission to call `load()` without clearing `total` or canceling an already queued advancement. ## v380 — Emberdeck Pilgrim 1.6: Fair Active-Time Ranking (BUILD ONLY; 2026-11-11)

**One bounded score/progression-integrity objective.** Exact v379 parent in Chromium used `(performance.now()-start)` where `start` was assigned at reset/page load. A monotonic accumulated-foreground timer settles on `blur` and `visibilitychange`, resumes only after focus/visibility return, prevents hidden UI actions from progressing the deck, and locks on completion. An aria-live clock description announces idle/active/paused/finished state. Restart resets the clock. Original combat, routes/relics, enemy intents, Legacy save, five-gate outcomes, defeat-unscored rule and terminal score formula remain; there is no timer or animation-loop dependency added.

# v327 — Catalog integration audit (2026-11-04)

**Single bounded objective:** Fix Neon Stack v326 mobile on-screen controls being nonoperational via focus/assistive activation, with focused Space triggering an unintended global hard drop. In baseline Chromium at 320×568 and 390×844, focused LEFT + Enter was ignored, and focused LEFT + Space dropped the active piece instead of moving left. This damages accessibility, scoring and gameplay integrity.

The existing internal action dispatcher and normal global Arrow/WASD/Space shortcuts remain unchanged. No third-party source, assets, dependencies or paid services were imported. # v326 — Skyhook Sprint 1.8: One Tap, One Grapple (BUILD ONLY; 2026-11-04)

**Single selected objective:** Repair Skyhook Sprint mobile HOOK double-dispatch, a game-breaking touch control defect. Real first-party v325 parent Chromium at 320×568 and 390×844 showed **one mobile tap called `useHook` twice**, attaching then instantly releasing the hook. The old handler treated `click.detail===0` as exclusively keyboard activation, but real mobile tap synthesized the same `detail===0` click. This meant the on-screen HOOK button could not keep the grapple attached.

**Original first-party change:** Replace parallel HOOK `pointerdown` and detail-gated `click` handlers with **one native button click handler** on release, supporting touch, focus Enter/Space and assistive semantic click. Existing E keyboard, B controller, jump press/hold/release, platform physics, checkpoint authority, pause/focus freeze and earned finish/ranking remain unchanged. On final first-party Chromium at 320/390 emulated touch, **one tap→one HOOK call with sustained attachment; next tap→one HOOK call and release**. No third-party engine/code/media imported; zero new runtime cost.

**Tests:** Four bounded 83-game chunks yielded 83/83 first-party Chromium startup/input/mobile checks. Focused browser tests covered pointer-only cancellation, semantic focus activation, keyboard E, synthetic gamepad B, pause, restart, one earned grounded score and no duplicate score. Baseline reproduction at both sizes: 10/10 expected-failure observations. Total bounded working-source validation: **944/944 assertions** across 12 grouped suites: Skyhook Sprint hook first-party Chromium 62/62, Skyhook Sprint prior physics/checkpoint/gamepad Chromium 23/23, Exact-v325 parent conservation 357/357, All 83 games Chromium startup/input/mobile smoke 83/83, Homepage desktop/mobile first-party Chromium 20/20, Astral Menagerie save browser regression 35/35, Orbit Breaker input/save browser regression 50/50, Imported keymap security Chromium 48/48, Host score precision Chromium 55/55, Run-history markup security Chromium 9/9, Service-worker mock lifecycle 19/19, Local HTTP precache resources 183/183. # WorldWideGames v242 / BUILD ONLY — 2026-10-25

## Tideglass Surveyor 1.2 — chart reset and competitive scoring integrity

**Exactly one objective:** fix the six-chart logic game's corruptible chart lifecycle. In the untouched verified v241 Chromium browser, solving chart 1, resetting, and solving the same chart again produced **2,400 instead of 1,200** points. Two stale asynchronous callbacks advanced two charts (chart 1 → 3), skipping an objective. A separate controlled test verified that an expired callback could advance a freshly restarted run. These are original first-party JavaScript/DOM observations; see `tests/v242_tideglass_parent_reproduction.json`.

**Original fix:** each chart grants at most one outcome, and queued auto-advance has an epoch token cancelled on restart. Confirmed or exhausted charts cannot be reset/re-awarded while transitioning. A chart restart is allowed before resolution, but retains cumulative misses and reduces the current chart's possible award by **200 points**; earned potential never increases through a retry. Winning after six distinct survey charts emits a single earned `survey-complete` score. The native focused Probe, Restart and Survey Again buttons accept Enter/Space without the global keyboard handler accidentally probing. Accessible live status announces confirmation and next-chart waiting. Original six fixed chart coordinates, Manhattan-distance lighthouse clues, four-probe attempts, correction penalties, touch and arrow controls remain.

**Focused browser verification:** 40/40 first-party Chromium checks (desktop arrow navigation, mouse, native keyboard focused buttons, emulated mobile touch, repeated inputs, nonrefundable misses, exhaustion, stale-callback cancellation, six real DOM-solved charts, and actual original parent platform score listener in iframe). Final real-host score **7,200**, exactly one local history row and one completion event. These are scripted/browser-emulated interactions, not human sessions or actual physical mobile hardware. Independently extracted final ZIP must repeat QA before release qualification.

**Source → mechanism → original implementation → evidence → rights:** `cxong/DrunkenViking` Git commit `356d8e19f27060e3330de73fa1e0c68accfc5c79`, `scripts/main.js` blob `8b428fc75dd8ae738f3c33c0ef33ad20f49f42c1` (level progression gated by replay state) and `scripts/scorecard.js` blob `7a7dcce64b9379a2afdbf26f4245f3242bc91e0d` (per-level result-based score). Tideglass original generation-token guard and earned-chart scoring; before/after controlled browser evidence above. Root code MIT, VT323 font separately OFL-1.1, art/audio separately unverified; **no third-party code, graphics, music, font or dependency imported**, no additional cost. Rejected adding a timing engine (too heavy for a two-callback puzzle) and a shared-host deduplication heuristic (could suppress legitimate runs).

# WorldWideGames v231 — Kiteglass Drift 1.1 swept gate scoring (2026-10-24; BUILD ONLY)

**One bounded critical gameplay objective:** Eliminate false clean gates and false misses caused by judging the aircraft's height at the end of a frame rather than the exact plane crossing. Verified against untouched v230 first-party Chromium: frame A actually crossed first gate at y=274.336 (84.336 from center, outside ±82) but scored it clean with zero penalty; frame B crossed at y=270.687 (80.687 from center, inside ±82) but incorrectly charged +6 seconds. Original goal remains seven gates, the same six-second penalties and lower-is-better time formula.

**Original fix:** Interpolate y at each crossed gate x between previous/current physics positions, checking the existing ±82 opening and ordered gates. Fast multi-gate steps are evaluated individually, reverse/no-cross steps receive no judgment, exact boundary passes, and result metadata includes a truthful clean-gate count. A live status message reports the actual crossing decision. No external gameplay code or assets.

# WorldWideGames v226 - Cinderline Velocity 1.4: Directionally Validated Checkpoints (2026-10-23; BUILD ONLY)

**Exactly one high-priority gameplay integrity repair:** Cinderline Velocity's v225 proximity-only checkpoint detection awarded gate points when a craft was parked on a reactor gate or travelled backward across it. Actual unmodified first-party Chromium reproduced both and permitted lateral crossings outside the gate posts; see `tests/v226_parent_checkpoint_repro.json` for the controlled fixture evidence. The original code used a radial distance `<5.2` test rather than a crossing test.

**v226 result:** The first-party racing simulation now validates a swept crossing of the **actual expected gate plane** in the forward track direction, within the visual post width, with positive vehicle speed. A high-speed legitimate crossing is detected by swept interpolation even if a frame ends beyond the old pickup radius. Failed/backward/stationary/outside-post traversals do not award points or alter the checkpoint order. Updated in-game instructions explain forward crossings; the original three race tracks, 2-lap rule, boost/drift/steering, rival motion, local time saving and ranked consecutive three-circuit championship remain unchanged. First-party QA forceGate/forceFinish fixtures now exercise the new legitimate swept crossing rather than a stationary proximity teleport.

Parent v225 already reconciles the separate Aegis v224 and Vanta v224 branches. Both are preserved; Cyber Wars 0.44 unchanged and dedicated Cyber Wars integration remains separate.

**Catalog:** 83 registered games, 129 distinct genre tags, 156 achievements, 55 remappable titles. Only `games/cinderline-velocity/index.html` among game executables changes; its `js/games.js` metadata is updated to 1.4. All other 82 games and their metadata must match exact v225. No externally sourced code, textures, audio, models, commercial fan-port data or API dependency was added. All changes remain BUILD-only, **not published**.

**Evidence (working tree, not final extraction):** v225 first-party Chromium baseline **7/7** including stationary/reverse/outside-gate false awards; v226 focused first-party Chromium **55/55** across all three track/gate geometries, 6 gate crossings for each course, exactly one earned championship event, desktop keyboard, touch emulation, performance callbacks and forced Canvas2D fallback; unchanged original legacy championship first-party Chromium **52/52**. Working-tree 45 real Chromium requestAnimationFrame intervals median/p95 approximately **16.7/16.7ms**, not actual hardware GPU FPS. Final independently extracted ZIP results are delivered in separate QA receipts and `TEST_REPORT.md` handoff; do not confuse working-tree success with final artifact certification.

**Source study:** Kart Royale source modules [`src/game/Race.ts`](https://github.com/ryancampbell/kart-royale/blob/main/src/game/Race.ts), Git blob SHA `1838bd19c3b42b87eba26af4b121d7a378c23398`, and [`tools/autoplay.mjs`](https://github.com/ryancampbell/kart-royale/blob/main/tools/autoplay.mjs), SHA `2d0484b2f54f08710c48f78c6063e6e77be546e5`, read 2026-10-23. # WorldWideGames v193 — catalog preservation and Emberfield integrity (2026-10-20; BUILD CANDIDATE ONLY)

**Exactly one bounded high-priority integrity objective:** scoring messages from Nova Burst, boss spawns, mid-run phase advances and unsuccessful survival attempts previously carried numerical `score` values that the unchanged WorldWideGames score receiver interpreted as ranked runs. The v192 first-party Chromium game+host reproduction showed **three false local competitive records** from one Nova/boss/loss sequence (7/7 assertions). A lost run could also overwrite the game's stored campaign-best score with 820 points. This was not authenticated online cheating; the existing local leaderboard remains editable.

**Fix:** only a finished **Pyre Regent campaign victory** emits a numerical competitive score. The original combat milestones, phase telemetry, earned award, survival mechanics, classes, keyboard/remappable controls, gamepad and touch remain. Unscored events carry a descriptive `attemptScore` metadata field and `ranked:false`, not a numeric `score`. A loss no longer overwrites campaign-best history; a terminal loss cannot later become a victory through stale completion processing. Visible result descriptions differentiate **RANKED CAMPAIGN CLEAR** from **UNRANKED ATTEMPT**. Historical local records are not retroactively deleted because past legitimacy cannot be proven.

**Chromium source-candidate tests:** 53/53 focused checks (one failed Nova/boss run produces **0** ranked scores; two mid-boss and final Pyre Regent wins produce **1**; quota-denied and security-denied storage still report the real win; replay legitimately produces one record per actual clear; gamepad synthetic and mobile-emulated touch/keyboard still operate), 265/265 catalog source-preservation assertions, 83/83 desktop game smoke, 83/83 mobile detail, 20/20 home/discovery, Orbit Breaker 50/50, Astral Menagerie save 35/35, imported keyboard-profile security 48/48, run-history escaping 9/9 and HTTP precache 179/179. Headless Chromium supplied 200 Canvas2D JavaScript draw-command submissions (median 0.0 ms limited by resolution, p95 0.2 ms), 55 browser animation callback intervals (median/p95 16.7/16.7 ms), 0 focused page exceptions. These are **not** GPU frame-rate measurements or physical-input latency. Terminal wins accelerated through first-party test hooks, not unassisted full play. Installed/disconnected PWA **NOT VERIFIED** because Chromium blocked localhost navigation with `ERR_BLOCKED_BY_ADMINISTRATOR`.

**Catalog preservation:** 83 games, 129 genre tags, 156 achievements, 55 remappable titles, 134 milestone and 81 completion event types. Emberfield is the only changed gameplay HTML; all **82 other registered games byte-identical**, Cyber Wars 0.44 unchanged, dedicated Cyber Wars 0.56 **not merged**. No new third-party assets/code, spending, or IP imports. Exact source parent is v192 SHA256 `21f96f1a0d1cd107108def67989d0569b1ba851ed04686abeb47121448fe36e6`; this is **archive ancestry, not a Git merge base**. Canonical main observed read-only `37f48c6ab75c47e89050ec7ee467cc87c0e8258a` (v72). No main/tag/Vercel/production write. # WorldWideGames v181 — Ironlight Breach 4.2 earned five-sector rankings (2026-10-19; BUILD ONLY)

**Exactly one objective:** Fix ranked score leakage from ordinary Ironlight Breach FPS events. Untouched v180 original game and WorldWideGames host in real Chromium reproduced an enemy-downed rank and then another ranked record after losing the run (**6/6 parent assertions**). The original `post()` always attached a numeric top-level `score`, so kills, secrets, cipher routes, vaults, intermediate `sector-cleared` and `breach-lost` could write browser-local ranked scores even without campaign completion.

**Implemented:** Intermediate gameplay events retain their original milestone names with `meta.attemptScore`, but have no root score. Only a fresh uninterrupted 1→2→3→4→5 sector campaign submits one earned competitive score on `campaign-complete`; death, checkpoint retry, explicit R restart, or skipped/isolated sector makes the remaining campaign **unranked practice**. Ordinary completions still count as actual clears, but cannot rank. An accessible `role=status` HUD indicator and visible in-game instructions explain eligibility. Duplicate `finish()` while locked cannot resubmit. Historical local score history was not silently deleted, and **browser-local records remain editable and non-authoritative**. Existing corridor maps, weapons, AI, bosses, cores, secrets, checkpoints, synth audio, keyboard/remap, mouse, gamepad and touch remain.

**Focused Chromium and host evidence:** v180 defect reproduced **6/6**; corrected actual first-party host+iframe game **47/47 PASS** (enemy and death unranked, partial sector 1–4 unranked, full earned five-sector win one exact match, retry-assisted and final-only practice unranked, duplicate guard, host-local reload, emulated touch, synthetic controller, accessible HUD, no script exceptions). Tests use scripted original game exits, **not** unassisted five-sector human play. 60 Chromium animation callback intervals median/p95 **16.7/16.7 ms**, **NOT** physical FPS/GPU or input latency.

**Bounded catalog:** original bundled game JS Chromium desktop smoke **83/83**, mobile-emulated game detail **83/83**, homepage/search/discovery **20/20** including Cyber Wars, Orbit Breaker **50/50**, Astral Menagerie saves **35/35**, imported keymap security **48/48**, run-history escaping **9/9**, precache resource HTTP **179/179**. # WorldWideGames v178 — Aetherfoil Circuit 1.1 earned championship score integrity (2026-10-18; BUILD ONLY)

**Exactly one bounded high-priority defect:** On untouched verified v177, Aetherfoil Circuit 1.0 sent a positive root `score` on ordinary `parry`, `bout-complete` and `perfect-bout` milestone messages; the actual unchanged WorldWideGames host recorded those as ranked scores before three-bout championship completion. Starting an unlocked final rival without winning prior bouts could also generate a false championship ranking. Original first-party game and original host reproduction passed **16/16** assertions and confirmed premature browser-local competitive results.

**Correction:** Preserve all three rivals, original parry/riposte/lunge/footwork physics, saving, unlocks, audio, keyboard/controller/touch, unranked milestone events and personal practice play. Store attempt scores in `meta.attemptScore`, not root `score`; require an uninterrupted, consecutively completed 1→2→3 rivalry sequence started as a new circuit. A defeat, bout restart or direct unlocked-rival practice makes the run unranked. A legitimate three-bout champion earns exactly one top-level scored `campaign-complete`; a final-only victory uses `circuit-practice-complete` with no ranked score or lifetime-clear increment. # WorldWideGames v159 — Rune Depths 2.3 local-run checkpoint integrity (2026-10-16; BUILD ONLY — NOT DEPLOYED)

**Exactly one bounded objective:** prevent malformed or user-edited browser-local Rune Depths records from fabricating a five-depth victory or mastery and causing invalid saves/crashes. The untouched v158 original game was reproduced accepting depth=5, runScore=888888888 and an impossible completion event with recorded score 888897208; invalid metadata also displayed an impossible 1/3 mastery. This is a locally editable browser persistence trust boundary, **not** evidence of remote account compromise or online anti-cheat effectiveness.

**Implementation:** Rune Depths **2.2 → 2.3** strictly checks the existing saved run and meta envelopes before applying game progression. It validates safe bounded integer score, turn, depth, HP and potion values; generated-maze walkability; player, enemies, gate, sigil and relic consistency; duplicate/unsupported entities; and mastery eligibility. Damaged checkpoints recover to a playable fresh delve with an accessible `role=status`/`aria-live=polite` notice; valid historical records and genuinely earned saves continue to resume. These are neither physical-device GPU FPS nor demonstrated performance gains. Inputs were actual Chromium keyboard and emulated touch plus synthetic standard controller. # WorldWideGames v157 — Aetherstead Colony 2.3 checkpoint safety (2026-10-16; BUILD ONLY)

**Single bounded objective:** prevent crashes, executable saved markup, and impossible restored turns/scores from malformed or hostile browser-local Aetherstead charter records. Against unmodified v156, a null cell crashed before any of 40 platforms appeared; a crafted building type or saved event-log entry executed injected `onerror` JavaScript; a saved turn of 999999 and campaign score of 999999999 were accepted. The controlled-snapshot Chromium reproduction is in `tests/v157_aetherstead_parent_repro_results.json`. This demonstrates unsafe local state hydration, **not proof of a remotely exploitable origin or of server score manipulation**.

**Implementation:** Aetherstead Colony **2.2 → 2.3** validates the complete local charter envelope before state assignment: charter/turn and completion relationship, finite bounded credits/resources/scores, approved civic policies, known building types and levels on exactly 40 platforms, valid cursor and actions. Invalid checkpoints fall back to a playable fresh charter with an accessible recovery warning; previously persisted valid lifetime seals are retained from the legacy mirror where available. Saved log entries are rendered using DOM text nodes (`textContent`, `replaceChildren`) instead of HTML parsing, preventing stored markup execution, and the five latest safe text entries reappear on reload. Historical legitimate in-progress and completed charters, keyboard/mobile construction, fair failure and exactly-once final campaign score, session-only storage denial, and the three-charter loop remain intact. No external packages, assets, models, rendering rewrite, paid services or new licenses.

HTTP precache resources **179/179 HTTP 200**.

**Measured browser data:** Aetherstead Chromium requestAnimationFrame scheduler **16.7 ms median / 16.8 ms p95, n=65** (headless desktop); historical regression 12 click-to-DOM actions median **33.68 ms**, p95 **49.54 ms**, not GPU frame time, hardware latency or claimed speedup. # Cyber Wars 0.27 — Coalesced guest movement backpressure (2026-10-13; BUILD ONLY)

**One bounded objective:** Prevent a viable co-op guest from being disconnected merely because its bounded relay mailbox contains redundant, already-superseded position packets. Baseline Cyber Wars 0.26 reproduced this behavior during a transient position burst; the corrected relay replaces unsent per-peer pose snapshots without exceeding the existing 12-message quota, losing ordered signal-state/peer-left events, or changing online economics. The independently verified latest catalog ancestor is v126, extended by Cyber Wars 0.26; the parent is an immutable archive, **not** a Git merge-base. Only Cyber Wars game metadata/title and relay are changed among registered games.

**Authority:** Core AGENTS.md and DESIGN_BIBLE.md at 8e9a3eeb05431d4410a9fb913d100f5ba13c25da were read before editing. No canon, world, economy, Pilot license, skill, saving, reward, death or governance changes; existing owned-home checkpoint remains the sole strategic save gate. Presence remains experimental and anonymous, NOT persistent public server-authoritative multiplayer.

**Research:** Python websockets broadcasting backpressure guidance (https://websockets.readthedocs.io/en/stable/topics/broadcast.html) explicitly discusses dropping/batching per-client messages. MDN WebSocket API (https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/WebSockets_API) documents lack of application-level backpressure. Adopted only a generic bounded latest-pose replacement pattern. Q3JS/Surfd and Oct 8 Radiant Optimizer's fan-port list remain benchmark-only; no proprietary implementation or retail asset imported.

**Release boundary:** A build-only, isolated WorldWideGames v126 + Cyber Wars 0.27 candidate, not an official WorldWideGames v127. # WorldWideGames v124 — Strict Local Scoring Boundary (2026-10-13; BUILD ONLY)

**Exactly one objective:** prevent browser-local scored-run histories from being created by coerced nonnumeric iframe messages. Real Chromium running the unmodified v123 `game-page.js` and an actual registered game iframe reproduced `score:null` as **zero**, inserting an ineligible best and scored history entry. The original receiver applied `Number(e.data.score)`; this also accepted booleans, whitespace, numeric strings, arrays and unsafe high magnitudes. This is a browser-local records/integrity defect, **not** proof of an Internet score exploit or an actual game naturally emitting `score:null`.

**Change:** `js/game-page.js` now accepts scores *only* when the active game frame submits a JavaScript `number` that is finite and rounds to a safe integer. Legitimate numeric zero and fractional scores remain supported; low-is-better games preserve their existing ranking direction. Unscored game events and milestones still count as before. The existing `source===frame.contentWindow` guard remains; the receiver also validates the reported origin on actual `message` events, with a local `file:` / opaque-origin compatibility allowance. Test harness events without a supplied origin retain prior historical compatibility. These checks cannot establish online authoritative scores or stop users editing their own browser storage.

**Parent:** immutable catalog v123 archive SHA-256 `6c4ac6dee7577ee1a21d023d7a5bda1004261d51c18d1c32552c5bffeed36856`; archive snapshot ancestry only, **not a Git merge base**. Canonical `ArchitectIndustries/World-Wide-Games` main observed read-only at `37f48c6ab75c47e89050ec7ee467cc87c0e8258a` (v72). New isolated versioned app resources `?v124` and `wwg-v124`. All **83 registered games** remain; all **83 gameplay HTML implementations are byte-for-byte preserved** from v123, including **Cyber Wars 0.23**, Moonwake Angler, Terrace Keeper, Astral Menagerie and Orbit Breaker. Catalog counts: 83 games, 129 genre tags, 156 achievements, 55 remappable titles, 134 milestone and 81 completion event types.

**Browser QA:** v123 defect reproduced in actual Chromium; v124 focused original-parent + Moonwake iframe 24/24 assertions (13 malformed score cases, legal zero/fraction/worse, foreign source and synthetic foreign origin, no page errors); Moonwake real parent 17/17 and gameplay 58/58; Terrace Keeper parent 18/18, gameplay 65/65 on unchanged-source retry after a transient missing-message assertion; 83/83 Chromium game interactions and 83/83 390px emulated-mobile objective/control detail pages, static catalog 133/133, Astral Menagerie persistence PASS, Orbit Breaker collision PASS, WebGL2/Canvas2D software fallback 16/16, and 180/180 HTTP precache GETs (178 distinct resources due two existing duplicates). Baseline and post-fix recordings in `tests/v124_QA_EVIDENCE.json`; independent extracted-final verification is separately recorded.

**Browser measurements:** score-boundary Chromium fixture 1.373s wall, 83 mobile game detail views 5.365s, software SwiftShader median JavaScript renderer submission 0.60ms (reference draw calls 254 vs instanced 37). These are neither real GPU FPS nor input latency/whole-game load time. Installed/disconnected PWA is **UNVERIFIED**: Chromium denied localhost navigation `ERR_BLOCKED_BY_ADMINISTRATOR`. Physical pad/phone, native Safari/Firefox, full unassisted 83-game playthroughs and public server score authority unverified.

# WorldWideGames v120 — Moonwake Angler 1.1 Honest Five-Fish Night Hauls (2026-10-12; BUILD ONLY)

**One bounded gameplay-integrity objective:** prevent Moonwake Angler timeout/partial-catch attempts from awarding the Moon Angler achievement or recording a competitive local score. Verified v119 immutable catalog parent `WorldWideGames_v119.zip` SHA-256 `fe4431e1e39501434142e32cfa6ade4245bb8fa267e0644852ef081c0940f887` (archive parent; **not** a Git merge-base). Canonical repository main read-only at `37f48c6ab75c47e89050ec7ee467cc87c0e8258a` (v72). 

**Real-browser reproduction:** unchanged v119 Moonwake Angler ended after 90 seconds with zero fish and sent `{event:'night-haul', score:0, meta:{catches:0}}` to its parent; the actual parent `game-page.js` records numeric scores and Moon Angler checks the `night-haul` event, so waiting without playing could falsely award completion and count an unsuccessful local record. This is a reproducible local progression/scoring defect, not an online leaderboard exploit.

**Implementation:** the original cast, bite, line-tension and five-fish gameplay remain intact. # WorldWideGames v118 — Windward Cargo 1.3: Reachable Routes & Honest Results (2026-10-12; BUILD ONLY)

**Exactly one bounded game-integrity objective:** make Windward Cargo's four-delivery campaign completable and its competitive outcome truthful. Starting verified parent is catalog **v117** ZIP SHA-256 `381e37fd4ce5bef3a359cd99a64426f906497e66932f0c231f6172de816687f9`. This ZIP is a **source-snapshot parent**, not a Git merge-base commit; canonical GitHub main has separate history and is read-only for this BUILD task.

**Reproduced defects in original v117 Chromium:** After a real first pickup/drop, exhausting fuel generated `route-ended` with numeric `score:1200` despite failure; the platform's actual game receiver treats this as browser-local competitive history. Two waypoint centers also intersected mountain collision geometry: second delivery `[1510,650]` was displaced to y≈522 (128px away, outside 48px delivery radius), and final pickup `[2330,650]` was displaced to y≈532 (118px away, outside 45px pickup radius). The complete route was therefore not reliably finishable. Browser test fixtures position the actual physics actor at these centers; they are not human flight playthroughs.

**Game source change:** Reposition second drop to `[1555,650]` and fourth pickup to `[2510,630]`, clear of the collision volumes, while preserving the original four routes, changing wind/altitude hazards, thrust/steering/fuel/cargo physics, synthesized audio and rendering. Complete four real pickup/drop detector cycles now generate exactly one positive `route-complete` scored event; failed fuel/cargo outcomes emit `route-ended` telemetry **without any `score` field**, carrying `meta.attemptValue` and `outcome='failed'`. A guarded terminal lifecycle prevents duplicate results or late failure→success. In-game instructions, HUD Attempt-vs-Score label, accessible role=status/aria-live result panel, keyboard R and new touch-friendly Fly again action make win/loss/retry understandable. Removed forced viewport zoom disabling to improve accessibility. No historical scores erased; browser-local records remain untrusted competitive indicators, not Internet rankings.

**Working-source QA:** Windward desktop/mobile actual Chromium **74/74** focused assertions including browser-controlled original pickup/drop physics, keyboard arrows/WASD, synthetic gamepad thrust/axis, emulated touch, failure with positive attempt points, both fuel/cargo failure, four completed deliveries, duplicate terminal calls, accessible result/restart, and the unmodified parent `game-page.js` score receiver with simulated browser Storage in a srcdoc iframe fixture. Parent denied loss, recorded one real victory, and denied another failure. Desktop/mobile Chromium 120 rAF samples each: medians **16.7ms**, p95 **16.7/16.8ms**. Browser scheduler intervals are **not hardware FPS or true end-to-end latency**. Child and parent no uncaught errors. Winner scores around 10,798 local points, loser 1,200 attempt points, with **zero** scored losing events.

**Catalog QA:** 83/83 Chromium boot/input/mobile emulation across four bounded 21+21+21+20 processes; 83/83 game-detail objectives/controls at mobile emulation (**6.144s**); representative unrelated gameplay **12/12**; static catalog contracts **127 assertions**; **179/179** HTTP precache resources (**1.076s**). Astral Menagerie persistence **35/35**, Orbit Breaker collision/progression **PASS**, Cyber Wars software WebGL2 and Canvas2D renderer **16/16 PASS** (requires virtual X server, SwiftShader JS submission median ~**0.50ms**, not GPU FPS). Scored outcome checks exercise exact game/parent JavaScript; other title smoke is bounded boot/input, not 83 full unassisted campaigns.

**Incomplete evidence:** Installed disconnected PWA **BLOCKED** on local origin by `ERR_BLOCKED_BY_ADMINISTRATOR`, and successful HTTP asset requests do not prove it. Actual hardware GPU FPS/memory, phones, physical gamepads, native Safari/Firefox, full unassisted four-route flight, real-origin IndexedDB/Storage persistence and any server-authoritative score validation remain unverified. Existing Cyber Wars WebSocket guests are experimental co-presence, not authoritative public multiplayer.

**Catalog/provenance:** 83 games, 129 genre tags, 156 achievements, 55 remappable, 134 milestone types, 81 completion types. **Windward Cargo is the only registered game source changed**; other 82, including Cyber Wars **0.21** and its Core Rank-10 Pilot mechanics, remain byte-identical to v117. # WorldWideGames v117 — Bastion Bloom 1.6 Honest Garden Outcomes (2026-10-12; BUILD ONLY)

**Exactly one objective:** fix a reproduced Bastion Bloom score-integrity defect on verified WorldWideGames **v116** (SHA-256 `79e1d21fdcad55c334d590b1d030fde2dda93c577088d891275b00a6072342c4`; ZIP parent, **not** a Git merge base). Unchanged v116's real first-wave enemy breach ended the game and emitted a **positive 798-point `garden-ended` loss score**. The existing WorldWideGames receiver accepts any finite `wwg:game-event.score` into the browser-local best and run history, even if the loss event is not a milestone. Thus a failed tower-defense campaign could misrepresent competition.

**Fix:** `games/bastion-bloom/index.html` now emits unscored `garden-ended` failure telemetry with `meta.attemptValue`, `meta.outcome='failed'`; only the legitimate ten-wave `campaign-complete` sends a numeric score and earns the existing Garden Warden milestone. Idempotent terminal guard prevents after-loss success or duplicate submissions. Mobile-visible `role=status`/`aria-live=polite` results, attempt-versus-score HUD labels, keyboard/touch restart, and explicit objective metadata distinguish ranked clears from unranked practice/failure. All original tower types, wave definitions and gameplay systems remain. No existing scores are retroactively erased. No server-authoritative leaderboard is claimed.

**Staging QA:** Real Chromium desktop 1280×800 and mobile emulation 390×844 completed **82/82 focused assertions**, including actual moving-enemy loss, ten in-game wave resolution handlers under controlled fixtures (not full human play), loss→win→loss, terminal idempotency, actual parent page's **unmodified scoring receiver** (srcdoc fixture only for blocked navigation), keyboard arrows/WASD, touch pointer, restart, outcome announcements and simulated Storage records. Positive loss attempt value **798**, scored synthetic win **23,373**; only the latter enters the browser-local run history. No uncaught errors. Animation 120 samples/viewport: medians **16.7ms**, p95 **16.7ms** desktop/mobile (browser scheduler, **not hardware FPS**). Catalog **83/83** interactive Chromium boot/keyboard/mobile in 21+21+21+20 batches, **83/83** details/controls/objectives (5.735s), **12/12** other gameplay interactions, **121** static assertions, **179/179** HTTP precache assets (1.067s). Historical Astral Menagerie persistence and Orbit Breaker collision/progression pass, Frostline gameplay **55** checks pass; Cyber Wars renderer WebGL2 and Canvas2D SwiftShader **16** checks pass after launching under Xvfb (median JS submission **0.6ms**). One first headed renderer attempt failed for lack of X server; this is recorded, not counted as success. `tests/v117_*` contains executable evidence.

**Limitations:** Functional installed/disconnected PWA **BLOCKED** by Chromium `ERR_BLOCKED_BY_ADMINISTRATOR` navigating localhost; HTTP asset 200s do not establish offline service-worker success. Parent+iframe score acceptance ran through real `game-page.js`, catalog/event rules, and in-browser `srcdoc` fixture due blocked URL navigation; browser Storage was instrumented for determinism and is not production-origin durability evidence. No physical gamepad (Bastion does not advertise native gamepad), actual phone, native Safari/Firefox, hardware GPU FPS, complete ten-wave unassisted human play, or public score anti-cheat verified. Cyber Wars WebSocket guest presence is not authenticated Internet-authoritative multiplayer.

**Catalog/provenance:** Preserve **83 registered games, 129 genres, 156 achievements, 55 remappable games, 134 milestone and 81 completion event types**. All **82 non-Bastion registered game implementations**, including Cyber Wars **0.21** and its authoritative Rank 10 Pilot license/progression, remain byte-identical to v116. Original game code/HTML messaging only; no imported protected assets, new code license, paid services, additional telemetry collection, public authority, or player data export. Cache namespace `wwg-v117` and `?v117` platform query references synchronized. **Build only:** canonical GitHub `main` `37f48c6ab75c47e89050ec7ee467cc87c0e8258a` read-only; no GitHub writes, tags, production promotion or Vercel deployment. ## v76 audit objective

Tetherline Salvage 1.0 correctly distinguished `haul-complete` from `haul-ended` for progression, but both terminal branches still attached numeric scores. Because the shared game page records any numeric game-event score, a partial timeout or hull-loss run could become the local Salvage best and enter recent scored-run history. No catalog expansion, external dependency, art/audio change, or provenance obligation was introduced.

## v75 audit objective

Aetherstead Colony 2.0 computed and banked district points before validating whether a charter succeeded. A failed charter could therefore inflate `campaignScore`, survive a retry, and submit a platform score under `charter-failed`; successful intermediate charters also submitted partial scores even though the catalog describes a Colony campaign score. Aetherstead 2.1 makes only fulfilled charters bank internal points and reserves numeric platform scoring for the completed three-charter campaign. No catalog expansion, external dependency, or provenance change was introduced.

## v73 audit objective

Gravity Foundry 1.0 emitted a scored `field-cleared` event on every one of its four fields and updated `wwg:gravity-best` immediately, so partial progression polluted score history while there was no explicit signal for completing the entire campaign. Gravity Foundry 1.1 preserves unscored field telemetry but makes `foundry-complete` the only scored true-completion event, emitted exclusively on field 4.

No catalog, art/audio provenance, external dependency, or licensing change was introduced.

## v72 audit objective

Harbor Pulse 1.0 used one scored `shift-complete` event for both legitimate 90-second survival and early reputation collapse. That ambiguity meant a failed run could set the game-specific best score while the platform had to leave the event unclassified to avoid false clears. Harbor Pulse 1.1 now emits neutral compatibility telemetry plus explicit `shift-cleared` / `shift-failed` outcomes. Only `shift-cleared` is a platform milestone/completion and only successful clears can submit a score.

## v71 audit objective

Vector League 1.1 used `match-complete` for every terminal outcome, so v69/v70 had to leave the event unclassified to avoid false clears. That protected losses and draws but also made genuine wins impossible to recognize as cleared. Vector League 1.2 emits explicit win/loss/draw outcomes, retains neutral compatibility telemetry, and submits a score only on victory. The platform classifies only `vector-league:match-won`; `match-complete`, `match-lost`, and `match-draw` remain non-milestone/non-completion signals.

No catalog, art/audio provenance, external dependency, or licensing change was introduced.

## v69 audit objective

Platform completion-integrity review found that v68 treated every positive `wwg:game-event` counter as a game clear. Real failure telemetry (`system-collapse`, `archive-desynced`, `regatta-dnf`) therefore appeared in the same cleared-game channel as legitimate campaign completions. The same unrestricted event stream also granted Daily Circuit milestone credit.

Platform 1.1 separates raw telemetry, supported milestones, and true completions through a shared event-rules module. A Chromium reproduction proved three failure-only events yielded three false clears before the fix; the v69 regression proves those same events now yield zero clears while legitimate side milestones and completions retain their intended behavior. Whole-catalog, objective/control, mobile, HTTP, and recent gameplay-regression gates remain green.

## v68 audit objective

Outcome-integrity review identified a Riftwake Regatta 1.0 false completion: the 105-second heat timeout invoked the same finish function as clearing gate seven. A reproduced zero-gate timeout emitted `regatta-complete`, submitted a numeric low-is-better score, and falsely reported seven checkpoints. Because Regatta Skipper listens to `regatta-complete` and the platform records any numeric game-event score, the bug affected both achievement integrity and local best-time integrity.

Riftwake Regatta 1.1 requires all seven gates for completion. Timeouts now emit `regatta-dnf` with actual checkpoint progress and no score field. Dedicated Chromium regression covers zero-gate and partial-course timeout, premature finish rejection, legitimate completion, saved remapping, and narrow mobile controls. The bounded catalog/platform gates remain green.

## v67 audit objective

Outcome-integrity review identified a Solar Loom 1.0 false completion: collapsing below the published 25-stability threshold still emitted `system-complete`, which could unlock the System Architect achievement. The same review found solar-shock penalties were immediately erased by stability recalculation and keyboard orbit selection omitted lane 6.

Solar Loom 1.1 separates stabilized completion from collapse, carries instability penalties through future calculations, and maps lane 6 to the `0` key. Dedicated Chromium regression passed, and the bounded 82-game interaction/objective/mobile plus HTTP/PWA gates remain green. Full evidence boundaries are recorded in `TEST_REPORT.md`.

## v66 audit result

The v66 quality objective came from completion-integrity review rather than catalog expansion. Pulse Archive 1.1 emitted `archive-cleared` whenever its song timer expired, including a reproduced 0%-accuracy, zero-hit run.

Pulse Archive 1.2 now requires at least 70% final accuracy for a successful synchronization. Lower-accuracy runs are explicit desynchronizations and emit failure telemetry instead of a clear event. Dedicated Chromium regression covers 0%, 60%, 70%, real keyboard timing input, and narrow mobile controls.

The completed 82-game interaction baseline remains green after the fix. This is evidence for the exercised states, not a mathematical claim that every possible future state is defect-free.

## v70 audit objective

Completion Coverage repairs a platform false-negative discovered after v69: nine existing success events were legitimate finite-game completions but absent from the explicit completion contract. The audit intentionally excludes ambiguous or non-success terminal events such as Harbor Pulse `shift-complete`, Vector League `match-complete`, Echo Bazaar `market-closed`, and Gravity Foundry per-field clears. Catalog count and provenance are unchanged.

## v121 Hearthline Dinner Rush score audit — 2026-10-12

83 registered games, 129 genre tags, 156 achievements, 55 remappable titles, 134 milestones and 81 completion types retained. Hearthline Kitchen v120 allowed an unplayed 0-plate timeout to emit scored `service-complete`; v121 requires at least three correctly cooked plates by a real 75-second deadline. Partial/failed shifts emit only unscored `service-ended`; achievement and completion filters preserve the earned-only `service-complete` key. Browser focus tests verify both terminal paths and actual platform score storage, while the 82 other registered gameplay sources, including Cyber Wars 0.22, remain unchanged. No third-party asset or rights change.
